XFor the Fations in a Horse never failing H1
A Pennyworth of Annisseed water the same quantity of Juyce
of HouseLeek well mingled put halfe into one eye & halfe in the
other eye the eares & watch the Horse 12 howers that he lye
not downe this will expel the Humors out of the body bycaus
ing the sore to runn abundantly the man that used this would buy
any Horse that had it at any reasonable price with the presumption
he could cure it with this

JohnJohanna StJohn

For a Bulock that pisseth Blood X B
The inwarrd Bark of an oke boyld in a qrt of raine
water to a pint & strain it when tis almost cold
put in a pennyworth of Bolarmonake give it blood
warme if once doe not give it 2 or 3 days over
againe

D X To cure any inward sickness or desease in a
Dog or a man & that is but with a mad Dog
A pint of new milk & 6 cl of  crabbs claws the black tipps of
them make them into powder then take rue bloodwort &
Liverwort each halfe a handfull boyle thes tel it comes to
3 qtrs of a pint then put in a spoonfull of good oyle the
like of Juyce of celondine & powder of Turmirick
it will serve three times for a dog fasting 2 howers
after it

I X For the Itch in man or woman or mang in a Dog
halfe a pound of flower of Brimston or sulphir & a qrt of
white wine put together give a wine glass to a man or woman
every night to a little dog 2 spoonfuls La nottlys experience

A
For An Ague Mr Woodhouse 3

4 ounces of Indian snake root pick it clean from the
dirt bruse it & put it into 3 pints of the spirit of sack
let it stand 6 weeks in the sun close stoped give 3
sponfuls an hower before the fitt keep yu bed all the
time your fitt lasts this usualy cures in 3 times but if a
quartan 6 times or more it is alsoe good for some
Feavors if the spirit be strong 2 sponfuls is enough
or if the person be a woman or a yong man after
you have spent this infution you may put into
the same rootes a quart of sack that will serve
poor people & children

For an Ague my Cosen Cutts

white pepper beat it & boyle it in viniger to a
poltis spread it on leather lay it in the hollow
of the foot between the heel & the foot doe it
halfe an hower before the fitt comes renew it
every fitt also the hairy side of a pece of the
skin of a Beare layd next the sole of the foot
has done many cures & Tarr spread on lambs
leather & Layd to the sole of the foot leting
it lye as long as it will stick is very good

A wrist medicine for an Ague

venes Turpentine spread on leather plaster
wise strew theron powder of olibanum mastick
& Frankensence bind it onto the wrists if it stick
it will quickly cure





For a Tertian Ague

Oyle of scorpians anoynt the soles of the feet & the
palmes of the hands & the forehead & the backbone
before the fitt comes

For an Ague in any swelling

A new pipkin put therin a quarter of a pint of salet oyl
the quantity of a walnutt of may butter the same
of Deers suit set it on embers & it let it not boyle
but simer a  quarte of an hour then take it ofe from
the fire stir it till it is almost cold then put in
a spoonful of english hony apply it warme to the
swelling

For an Ague

The Hart of a Pike taken out & drunk in maligo

For an Ague in a sore a Poltise

Two quarts of Runing water malows and smalage each
2 handfuls red Fennel & sage each a smal handful
chop your Herbs smal boyle them in a pot with some
Linseed about 2 handfuls & the same of otemeal
a little brused a little vineger a pint of red wine
leez a pint of milk from the cow almost a pound
of suit shreed smal boyle them til it be thick like
a plaster lay it as hott as it may be suffred

For an Ague swelling

Oatmeal boyle it in new milk to a poltis then adde
to it may Butter & Aqua vitte if it be a sore lay this
on top of a Plaster hott

A Poltis for the wrists in a Ague

Aqua vitte mustard seed & Rye meal beat thes
into a poltis lay it on a little before the fitt in 3 tims
it will cure also to hang a pece of camphir as big
as a walnut in a Bag about the neck til the Ague
be gone

For the swelling to the Legs coming of an Ague
Elder & Bloodwort leaves each a Handful Bay salt & roack
Alom each a suffitient quantity fry thes in Boars greac make
a poltis or plaster of it & lay it to the Leggs

For an Ague Tertian

A Head of Garlick peel the cloves boyle it in haffe a pint
of sack tender then take it out & bruse it & put it in
againe then put it into as much of the sowerest leven as
will make it into a poltis spread it on 2 cloths lay it hott
to the sole of each foot 2 howers before a fitt goe to bed
when yu lay it on let it lye 12 howers use it 3 fitts

Dr willis his wrist medsine for an Ague

Hopps curans & Bay salt each alike quantity beat
them to a poltis lay it almost round the wrists sowe
it on pritty hard a little before the fitt comes

For an Ague or swelling in the Brest

new milk red rose leaves Brown sage each a hand-
ful chop them boyle them tender with a pece of sheeps
suit then put in a little Flower lay it on hott this
will dissolve or break it

Dr willis to drink in a Ague

Rough Dent de leon either boyld in poset drink or
the juyce in white wine

Another for an Ague

Take rootes of yarrow wash & chop them put them
in a silk bag as big as the pawm of ons hand hang
it to the pitt of the stomach chang it 2 howers be-
fore the fitt doe this 3 fitts this yeare many Agues have
been cured with a plaster of Galbanum to the navel & some with
eating a magpye baked in a pye 1680





Dr Willis for long Agues wch succeeded a
Feavor & caused in them great & hard Bellys
a drink & Oyntment

Take Lavander cotton Romon wormwood centuary carduus
each a smal handful the peel of 3 oranges & i
Lemon cloves & mace brused each a spoonful chop
thes & stew them in a pound of fresh butter & halfe
a pint of white wine 2 howers til the wine is all
spent strain it & keep it in a gally pott anoynt the
sides & belly mornings therwith & keep quilts
smeared therwith upon it

The Drink

Camomile Carduvs, wormwood Horehound centuary
Brooklin watercresses scirvie grass each 3 handful
the peel of 10 oranges chop all thes put them in
a galon of cyder poset drink stil them in a comon
stil til it begin to tast sowr put first & last to
gether take 10 sponfuls with suger first & last

For an Ach in the Huckle Bone caled the sciatica

Black sope one pound a pint of Aquavitte halfe a pint
of salet oyle a qtr of a pint of juyce of Rue boyle &
stir them on a soft fire till they be somwhat thick
so as to be made into a plaster spread some of it on a
pece of Leas\ther apply it hot to the place letting it
lye 3 days & 3 nights unmoved if one cure not lay
on another it is most excellent

For an Ach or Lamenes

Ducks meat that grows in the water & moss that
grows on the sail of a windmil boyle them in dreggs
of Ale like a pece of beafe til it be so thik that it
will spread than spread it on Fries a dow red culor &
the thickest lay it below the part greived bind hot
clothes about it let it lye 2 nights & dayes keep
yr bed all the time & sweat if you can



A:4

For any Ach or the Sciatica

Great earth wormes strip them toward the Tayle till all the earth
be squeesd out of them rub them clean in a linnin cloth put 3 scor
of them into a pint oyl boyle them til they crackle then cast them
away anoynt the place with the oyl against a soft fire

For an Ach in the Flesh or Bones

Smalage Bawm ground Ivie camomile comphry rootes each a handf
chop them smal & boyle them in may Butter put in a sponful of mace
strain it & put in a little saffron beaten

For an Ach or numnes or lamenes in the Armes

The Ashes of Ashen wood steep them all night in water power of the
cleerest boyle in it sage rosemary camomile bays each a Handf &
with Flannel or cotton dipt in it bath & foment the Armes as one
cooles dip another for halfe an hower then wipe it very dry &
wrap it up warme so doe every night til the party be well

For An Ague

make cakes with Aque vitte & wheat Flower & lay to each wrist one
raw not baked & within the bending of the Arme wher they let
blood bind them hard on an hower before a fitt

For an Ague

Take 6 right civel oranges pare the outward rhind as
Thinn as is posible put it into a pint of true sherry sack
give 2 sponfuls night & mornings the well dayes & also
the fitt day & an hower before the fitt this alsoe is good
for the wormes

For an Ague +

A dram of mirrh in powder give it in a spoon
full of sack an hower before the fitt & a spoonful
after it tis certain at twice or at 3 times failes not

Dr Tabors Ague medicine
Cortex of Peru 4 drams extract of gentian
roote one dram contra = yerva verginian snake,
weed & sassafras of each halfe a dram make all
thes into an electuary with Hony this quantity
is to be taken at six times upon the two well dayes
morning noon & night about the bignes of a wal
nutt after euery Dose drink a glasse of good wine
of any sort it is to be taken againe within a week
to prevent relapsing

Dr Mathias for the whites & weaknes in the Back

Venes Turpentine very well washt in red rose water
swallow euery morning as much as a Hazel nutt roled in
powder of nutmeg & suger fasting an hower when you
are weary of this take as much powder of mastick
as will lye on a 6 pence in an egge rear rosted vse
this a fortnight

Dr Browns Clary Water for the Back

3 gallons of strong Beere or Ale put iy in a brass pot that
will hold 4 gallons 10 handfuls of clary gathered in a
dry day 3 pound of stond raisins Liquorish & Annieseede
each 3 pennyworth the whites of 24 eggs
beat the shels smal & mingle them with the eggs the
--- bottoms of 3 manchits distil it in a limbech

For a Bone Ach excellent

A pennyworth of Brandy as much of oyl de Bay mix
& warm them chafe it in well against the fire keep
the part warme

it on 2 fingers from the bile & so on till it comes to the
place wher you would have it break

To take out the fire of a Burne speedyly

To ripen a sore Brest

The cream of new milk manchitt grated very fine
the yolk of a new layd egge gilgoby the ground a
handful shreed very fine stir them all the while til
ther be like cream on the Top of it then spread
it and apply it as hott as may be indured shifting
it 2 times a day when it is broke Tent it

For to disolve a Brest tho never so hard

A pint of vrin a good handful of marsh mallows if yu
cannot git them ordinary malows boyle it till halfe is con-
sumed then put in as much barlye meal as will make it
into a Poltis let it boyle a while then put into it a bitt
of Hoggs greace as big as a walnutt take it ofe & put
in a sponful of Hony, lay it on as hott as can be suffred
let the part take 12 Hoglice brused in white wine
3 sponfuls euery morning

A Custart for weaknes in the Back

The yolks of 4 new layd eggs the pith of an oxe Almon
beat them thurowly & strain them with a little new
Cream that is not aboue 8 howers old lay the pith
in water to take out he blood & strip it from the
skinns put therto nutmeg rose water & suger
either make it into pudings or bake it wch yu
will also eggs potched in water wherin mint has
been boyled also the milts of all fresh fish is good
for the back if the patient be cold in his back
oyl of camomile capons greace & sanders boyle it
in a pot in a pot of water anoynt the back
Lay on a blew cloth that is wodded lay it next
the skin
If they be hott in the back take oyl of roses the
seeds of Plantaine & the yongest Plantaine
you can gitt & some sanders boyle thes in a gally
pott in a skillet of water anoynt the back &
wear a blew cloth this will cure the back tho
in a consumption

For any Knob or hardnes in the Brest or milk
Guard

A pint of Rope oyle a qtr of a pound of white
lead beaten very fine & as much red lead beat
fine shomakers greac qtr pnd halfe so much
virgins wax boyle all thes in the oyle on a soft fire
take it ofe if it ofer to run over. let it boyle til it look
brown then put in halfe an ounce of camphir
cool it in water make it into roles spread it when
yu vse it on the fleshy side of sheeps leather let
it lye til it drop ofe

cut them in halfe & with a pen knife scrape out
all the seeds & downe let them lye 2 days couer-

ed in an erthen bason beat & Pulp them throw

For the Bitting of a mad Dog never failing

Leaves of Rue clean picked 6 ounces peeled
garlick 4 ounces methridate or London
Treacle 4 ounces shaveings of Pewter 4 spoon-
fuls a pottle of strong Ale put thes into a ves

fal or cold

Susan Foot for a Burne or Scald Blast Blite
or St Antonys Fire

Pikked Ivie that runns upon the ground & gilgoby
ground each 2 handfuls sinkfoyl the same chop all
thes smal with a pound of hard Kidney mutton suit
stop thes hard into a pipkin let it stand 3 days boyle
it very gently putting in 3 sponfuls of snow water
boyle it till the Herbs are crisp strain it hard into
a pennyworth of salad oyle onoynt it stil as it
drys up tel the paine be gone then lay on a rag
dipt in to the burne or scald shifting it often

B:6:

Mr Boyles Balsame of sulphire

Take one part of Flower of Brimstone & fiue of pure
oyl olive mingle thes together well set them on a gen-
tle fire in an earthen or a silver pan not very deep
but pritty flat & wide let it not boyle but simper & keep
it still stiring with a silver or glasse spoon tell such
time as the flower of Brimstone by fully melted in your
oyl wch when it is it will look very Red then take it
ofe the fire & let it stand a while & coole then set
it on againe still stiring it tell it be thurowly melted
& grown pritty thick like a Balsame or oyntment then
keep it close couered for your vse

this mingled with oyle of Annus of Fennel seeds & fine pow-
der of white suger candy a little taken on the poynt
of a knife is an excellent remidy for a cough

Alsoe one part of this & 3 parts of oxycrowshom plaster
& applyd warme to the Kings euel swelling if not broken
will desolve it & the Balsame alone when it is broken if
dropt warme into it & some applyd on lint warme to it
once or twice a day cures it for vlcers in the Lungues
to take the fire out of Burnes the best war for inward
takeing it is to make it with oyl of eggs & for outward
applications with oyl of St Johns wort

For Bleeding at nose

A sheet of white Paper wett it in vineger & dry it
in an oven when it is dry well it againe & dry it as
before so doe 3 times then make it into powder & snuff
up some of it into the nose oftin as well when it
dos not as when it bleeds





For a Cancer in the Breast

A piece of a sheep skin taken ofe the Flank
of the sheep lay the skinn side to the
breast changing it once in 12 hours & in
hot weather once in 6 howers this was
used to a woman whose breast was to be cut
ofe & but was not broke .... & it kept her very
many years without any pain or trouble
& at last died of another desease La child
knew the woman to whom it was taught
by a French man

Another for a Knubb or hardness in a Breast

A piece of Brown Paper dip it in Brandy
dry it & spread it ouer with Honey Lay
it to the breast & in a months time it
will disolve it it must be new once in
a day or two

For a Cancer in the Breast

A print of chickin broth 12 or 15 crafish
alive beat them in a morter & put some
of the broth to them beat them & strain
them as you doe Almons to make Almon
milk beating & straining them til you have
all the goodness of the crayfish out drink it
euery morning a pint

The Lady Barkshirs famos cancer water to
prevent +

Take Lapis callaminaris 4 ounces make it red Hott
& quench it in a pint of white wine heat & quench it
so 9 times til the wine boyle up to the Top of the
cupp wch must be silver & it must stand neare the
fire but not to touch it then take an ounce of
Tutty burne it in a cruciple Red hott quench it in
a little Red Rose water double stild a little will quench
it so doe 3 times then put a pint more of double
stild Red Rose water with that you quencht the
Tutty in into the wine beat the calliminaris &
the Tutty severally very fine then put both to
the wine & Rose water bottle it & shake it twice
a day

For Deafnes

X  For a Dropsey D

The Topps of yong Broom burnt to Ashes

To temper and cleans the Bloud.

Take the Berries of Elder when they are full ripe
and pick them from the stalks then put them in
a pitcher and Infuse them in a Kettle of water,
then take a Gallon of the juice and putt it into
a Pipkin, then Infuse it till it has wasted about
a quart, then fill it up till twelve Gallon comes
to one.

It must be stirred with a wooden Slice for fear of
burning, and take one spoonful morning and
Evening.

Blosoms Angelico rootes 4 ounces Polipodium of the oake a
pound slice the rootes & put thes flowers as they are
in season into a larg Bottle with 3 gallons of sack
keep it stopt close but let it be big enough other

it put in also 4 ounces sinomon the same of
sweet Fennel seed & nuttmeg & Cardamum seeds
all brused the peels of 12 oranges & the same of
Lemons when thes have stood 3 or 4 days after

Flowers & the new sack & stil the old sack

Dr Allin For Deafnes

To know the 4 causes of Deafnes
the first is an obstruction in the orafice or Hole
of the eare wch is either hard & Black or Blackish
& clamy upon the Dossel of cotton wool silk or lint
the wch must be twisted upon the end of the
probe

The 2nd is an impstuma of a greenish culor
& unsavory smel at the end of the probe

the 3d is a Bladder of water at the hole of
the eare like the Bladder of a Roach

The 4th is a congelation it is known by the
Dimnesse drynes of the Drum head or Timpanum
or greynes of it & they that are thus trobled
have always a noys in ther head & wind & no
eare wax at all but the eare is perfectly dry
sumthing scaly like Dandrew to know the Berrys
wherof the Balsame is made to cure this 4 causes
as also surfitts Bruses Rissing of Blood runing of
the reighns inward deseases coming of inflamation s
or impostumations of the stomach or any other part
they grow by river sides & in moyst places upon a
long stalt or stalks by Ditches or ponds in clusters
or Bunches like cherys being perfect red in culor
haveing fine little smal Iagged leaves like straw
Berrys but in culor blewish & the stalk blewish
ther are 4 kinds of berys like thes but they
have but 3 or 4 stons apece wher as thes have 30
or 40 stones in each of them & a perl on the Top of
euery one of them they must be gathered in August
or september beware of Bruseing them in the
gathering





put them into an earthen vessel couer them with
water boyle them til they swim but break non of
them strain the liquor from them without breaking
any of them then strain the Berrys thurow a
reasonable thick cloth & strong for feare the seeds
should come thurow this done put the Balsame
into some close vessel

To cure the first cause caled an obstruction

make an iniection with one pint of faire
water & halfe a pint of red rose water & 3
or 4 drops of Balsame infused into the iniection
water & when you dress the patient powr
some of the water into the porenger make
it as hot as yu can well suffer it on the back
of your hand when you iniect it into the
eare wch doe i0 or ii times together at
one dressing wth your sering or wind in-
strument Lay a napkin on the neck of the
patient & hold a porenger to the eare to re-
ceive the iniection & obstruction then clens
the eare very dry dress the patient with
the Balsame a dossle being dipt halfe way
therwith & put into the eare as farr as the
patient can indure it leave it therin 24
howers this with gds blessing is cured in 4
days

to cure the 2nd kind

make yr iniection with Beife Brine & sack
Lees boyle & scum them strain them throw
a cloth 4 or 5 times double then drop into
a pint of it 5 or 6 drops of yr Balsame & make
iniection with yr sering & draw what wind

The 3d cause

Cut a pece of a spung as big as a finger & an
Inch long wett it well in rose water then bind

The spirits to make Usquebah must be drawn

F:3: 56

To know a Fracture or whither the scull be broke

Take a packthrid tye a knot at one end & give the
party the knott between his teeth let him shutt his
teeth close & hold it fast take the other end in yr
Hand hold it straight as if you would pul it out of the
patients mouth while you pul it straight with one hand
give it a twitch on the midst of the string with the
other hand that may make the third twang as yu
doe the string of a viol when you try if it be in tune
doe it pritty hard if the party cannot endure it to be
done twice but thinks it like a Dagger in his Head
it is a Fracture but if he can endure it the scul
is not crackt alsoe they vsually find paine in set-
ing ther teeth together or chewwing meat & are sub-
iect to vomiting

For the Faling Sicknes

The fairest earth wormes one quart put them into a gallon of spring
water shift it twice in 24 howers then drain them from the water
in a seive then lay them by one in a larg dripping pan turning
them 2 or 3 time by a reasonable fire tel they be dry enough to powder
serce them & keep it in a viol close stopt it will keep a yeare
take a sponful when you find the fitt coming like wise morning & euening
3 days before & 3 after the chang of the moon & so after & before the full
moon abstain from onions garlick & salt meats this will cure in 3
months if not continue longer for tis a certaine remidy the best wormes
are found in a Tanners miskin tis good for pain in the stomach

An Excellent receit for the Goute

Two pound of May Butter unsalted put it in a new
earthen pot well glazd & boyle it gently ouer a fire
of oaken wood but by noe means cole or Turfe or
any sulphurious wood or fuel put into it 25
the greenest & largest Froggs can be got put in
two or three at a time tel they be all in let
them boyle tell they are boyld to peces then strain
them into another pot then break the flesh &
bones very smal in a morter then take Ars-
smart Burrdock rootes & leaves & walnutt tree
leaves cutt them smal & beat them in a morter
put them & the Froggs into the foresaid Butter
boyle them well & straine them & cast them
away & when the salve is cold put a knife
thurow it to the bottom & let the water out
that is in the bottom tye it up close it will
keep a great while
herewith bath the party wher the paine is before
a fire with oakenwood rub it well in tel the paine
ceaceth afterwards only once a day then once in
two days then once in 3 days & after once in 4
then only in march & April october & nouember
bath it 5 or 6 times spring & fal he need not
feare cure ther must be a handfull of each of the
root & Leaves aboue mentioned

X Dr Lowers advice in a Fitt of an Appoplex + A

Desolve 20 graines of Resin of scammony in 2
spoonfulls of Palsy water or Plague water when it
is desolved you may adde 2 sponfuls of any smaler
water to make it not to strong then give it all at
once & give the folowing Glyster presantly
A pint of the decoction of sage Rosemary &
marierom & Lauander Flowers adde to it syrop
of Buck thorne 6 spoonfulls 4 sponfulls of the in-
fusion of crocus metalorum the same you give
for vomits apply cupping glasses with scarifica-
tion to the shoulders cup so often after the
scarifing tell you draw 8 ounces of the Blood by it
beside wch bleed 8 ounces out of the Arme &
blow some white Hellibore powder into the nose
apply blisters to the sholders ou above the
Issues if they have any if the Resin of scammony
doe not work in 4 howers give i0 graines more
disolved in the same manner

other for halfe an hower

Water of swallows for the Head

It is good for the pation of the Heart mother

Lethargy any thing coming from the Head comforts
the braine tis good for distraction light Headednes
in a feavor midsomer is the best time

For the Black & yallow Iaundice Lo Burlington

once takeing at twice it has not been known to faile
resting two or 3 days between

For the Iaundis

Take a quart of mild strong Beer boyle & scum it then

To Make Gascon's Powder.  P

Take of e Claw of e Crab shell being beat to powder as fine as
you Can gett it, & sift it through a Tiffeny searcher, take of it
4 Ounces & take of the best seed Pearl, & of red Corall of each
1/2 an Ounces beat e Powders seu'rally, & do them ^through a very fine searcher
then put e Pearl, & Corall to e Crabs Claws & mingle it very well
together, then make it into a paste wth a Ielly of Snakes skins adding
to it a little Rose water that hath had Saffron in it, to give it a
tincture, then work it well together upon an earthen plate with
a knife a little att a time till you see it look close, so make it
into balls, & dry them in a Stove, or before the fire.

The Crabs are to the taken when the Sun & Moon are both in
Cancer wch happens but two days in a year, & they must be quick
and dead in e time, all the Claw is good but the top that is black
is e best

To Make Ielly of Snakes Skins.

Take ten or twelve Snake's skins as they are in bigness, Cut
each into 4 or 5 pieces & tye them loosly together with a thread
put these into a pipkin leadred within, that will hold about a
Pottle of spring water, then sett them on a Charcoal fire &
let the Pipkin be Cover'd, all but a little vent, stir if often
with a spoon to make the scales rise, at e top, when you see
the scales & a black scum rise att e top take it off, then there
will rise a white Scum which you must not take off when you
see the skins so tender as to Come to pieces, strain all through a strain-
er squeeze it very hard till the black appear through the strainer



then leave off & put the liquor in a less pipkin leaded within
& Covering it Close let it stand simpering on Coales till it Iellye
then put in the rose water & saffron will serve to colour it, &
warm itt when you use itt. Keep a Constant gentle fire under itt
lest the water Consume before the skins be tender.

Mrs Howlands way to make oyl of Charity

take of the leaves of Rosemary Camomile Lavan-
der wormwood & Red sage each 4 ounces chop
them smal put them in a glass put so much oyl
olive to them as will well wett them & swim a little
on the Topp cover the glasse & let it stand a
month in the sun string it often then strain
all those Herbs from the oyle then take 6 ounces
of euery one of the same Herbs you had before
& 4 ounces of valerian chop them smal & put
them into the same oyl out of wch yu strained
the other boyl it on a gentle fire stiring it
continvally least it burne when it is boyld enow
strain it & let it stand 3 days in a Glass vessel
then powr out all the oyl & boyl it half an hower

To make Boyld Conserve of Red roses for Ruhm & Consumption

halfe a pound of the best budds clipt form the whites a pound &
a halfe of loaf suger beat them very fine set thes by then take
halfe a pint of spring water or red rose water set it on the
fire in a quart skillet then put into it as many rose leaves
with the whites as you can cram into the skillet put but
a little fire least it burne then stir the leaves down now &
then till they are very white then strain them & put in so ma-
ny more so doe three times euery staining they will looke
lesse white then other the last time put in your clipt
Budds let them be tender then strain them & beat them till
they be as fine as papp & while the Liquor is hott put in
the suger into the skillet & put in yr roses when they
are fine stir them very well to make the suger melt
then take them ofe before it boyle & stir them well tel
all the lumps both of the suger & roses be desolved pot it up
it will have an Ice candy wch will keep it all the yeare it is
best for consumptions wher they are apt to loseneses

For a watry Rupture in the Codd of a Boy

of the same person being so the nurse had a new milk
that purged it & euery time the nurse opened it she
would gently shake the codd upward & the child would
presantly voyd wind & water & so it went away in a
little time

A Poltis to aswage any swelling & festred sore

keep a fortnight

Cap Greens Red Powder for the same cure

White coperis 4 ounces Camphir an ounce beat thes both
severaly put the camphir first into a little black cup put
the coperis upon it cover the cup with a paper & a
cawcer & lay on a weight set the cup into a chaffing dish
of smal cole keep a soft fire  to it for 3 or 4 howers & in
that time it will be hard enough to take out of the pot with
out breaking the pot then beat it with 4 ounces of Bolarmonake
beat them & serce them together make it up in ounce & halfe
ounce papers one ounce will make a pottle of water put it in the
water to warme & scald not boyle put it in a glasse towarms settle

plaster next it & in cace of great paine the Red ouer the green

The Yalow Salve or Flower of oyntments

Deers suit & olibanum each 4 ounces mastick

or dish clout wett in dish water) into another such

suit or oyle & alsoe 2 quarts of right white wine

elce it will burne it will boyle 8 howers if the wine
be good it must boyle till the wine be consumed wch yu may

warme then put it in a pail of cold water & work it into roles
which keep it in oyly paper euery role by it selfe

Syrope of Corral

A pint of cittron Iuyce & put into it an ounce & a halfe
of corral finely serced & powdred & let it stand upon
the sand where your stils are about i4 days till it
hath goten a good corral culor from the corral
stiring it 2 or 3 times a day with a wooden spattle
then take the rhinds of 4 oranges & 2 lemmons
very thinn pared & halfe a pint of Dammask rose
water put thes in a close glasse stil & lute it
close with Bladder & set it in the water to stil
& it will be done in i4 days & nights filter &
straine it from the peels & then boyle it with as much
hard suger as will make it a syrope & by degrees put
in the tinctur of corral being filtered alsoe

Spirit of Lemmons Lady Compton

50 civel Lemons fresh & well colured pare the peel in bitts
no biger then a penny & so thinn put then into a glass or
wide mouthd Iugg put to them 6 quarts of the best cannary
stop it close let it stand 6 dayes draw ofe a quart in a
Limbech in wch steep for 6 days more the thinn pareings
of i2 or i4 more Lemons stoping them close then take 30
Lemmons more beat the yalow of the peels in a marble
morter squees out the Iuyce upon halfe a pound of white
suger candy beat & scerced then strain the distilled
water upon the candy & Iuyce if they will not mixe put
in some very close thing & set it halfe an hower in hot
water & let it run thurow a cotton Bag & bottle it

Spirit of Harts Horne Mr Boyls way

of Harts Horne broken upon an Anvile in peces as big
as a mans finger put a pound or a pound & halfe or
2 pound at most according to the bignes of the vessel
into a glasse retort the stronger & larger the better
place the retort in a sand furnace & give a very
gentle fire 5 or 6 howers tel the phlegme appere in
great drops upon in the recever wch must be well
fastned on & large then increace your fire by
degrees tel you se a volatile salt stick in the
neck of the retort & the sides of the reciever
continue that degree of Heat tel you se noe more
salt come ouer & then increase it as much as yu
please wherby you will force ouer a darkish red
oyl of wch I seldome make much use & therfore
when about a spoonful of that is come ouer I leave
ofe the distilation take away the receiver & wash
down all the volatile salt with the phlegm &
salt spirit then powr them alltogether with the
oyle into a Tunnel with a narrow hole wherby yu
may easyly seperate the oyl from the rest that wch
rests must be set to rectifie in Balneo or any such
gentle heat in a tale Head & body the higher the
better if you give not to strong a heat ther will first
assend into the Head a clean volatile salt wch if you
please you may take out of the Head & keep by it
selfe but because it is not easy to git nor keep it
nor give it I vsualy continve the rectifiecation til
the liquor that arises up have washt down all the salt
& carryed down the rest with it into the reciever which
I then take ofe

For a Sore that will not Heale Dr Lower

Wet a rag in fasting spittle then take up a little quick
silver upon it & rub ouer a pece of Lead beat very thinn
tel it be silvered with it & apply it to the sore nothing
between & doe it new ouer with quicksilver
euery 2 days & purge once in 2 days while you
vse it this cured a Leg of mine that had been many
months ill in i2 days after I had tryed all ordinary salves
& alsoe cap Greens Powder

Syrope of steel Dr Lower gave me & wch
he vses

An ounce of Filings of steel infuse it in a qrt of

Mrs Highlords Breast Salve 187

Take a new pipkin and put in a Pint of e best Sallet
oyl, and 4 ounces of yellow Bees wax slict thin; &
set it over a slow clear fire & keep it stirring
till it be ready to boyl, then take it off and
stir it till it be a little cool, then put in two
spoonful of e best honey, & when it is well ming-
led set it over the fire, & put in 6 ounces of
Diaculum with gums, slict very thin, and when
it is well mingled keep it stirring till it be quite
cold, and keep it close covered for your use

When you use it for a Breast spread it thick and
dress it twice a day, if e humour will disperse it
will do it, or break it wth more ease than any
thing you can use and let e Patient drink
good store of Baulm Posset drink, & refrain Salt
meats & strong liquors, it cures old ulcers, Sore
legs, St Anthonys Fire, Tetters, all swellings red
& white, & a most excellent salve for e Kings= evil
and green wounds, when there are holes tent them
wth the salve

Dr Sheafs water for an Old sore

Fil an Earthern Pipkin holding tow gallons with the herbs mer-
cury gathered erly m the mirning & also before it be seeded
put to it 6 quarts of running water out of a spring against the
east & set it on a constant fire with an earthern pipkin of
4 quarts of the same water to fil it up as it seeths away & til
5 of the tenn quarts were boyld away stir down the Herb with
a stick when it is thus halfe consumed take it ofe & with the
stick drane out all the herbs into an empty pipkin & crush them
between 2 Trenchers into the licquor let it stand till next
morning close couered & put two quarts of it into thegallon
pipkin with a qr of an ounce of sublimed mercury & as
much of the same mercury into the liquor m the great pip-
kin with 3 qrs of an ounce of Alome put noe Alome into
the lesse pipkin set them both on the fire keeping them
both stiring wiht a stick tel they boyle up then take it ofe
& when it is quite cold fil it out into strong glasses if it
be not stired that which is on the Top will be the mildest
& the bottom the sharpest.
Make good store of the Herb water m Aprel & may without
the nettles some sores this water will cure & it is vsed to
qualifie the sharper water as occasion serves the oftener
a sore is dressed the soner it will be well apply noe salve
only wash the sore with Ducks feathers Dipt in the water
& lay on the smooth sides of a mercury leafe or a rag
dipt m it with a rag alsoe when you take it ofe wet
the rag least you bruse the wound in taking it ofe.

To make the Perfume for Sr Tho Ivies Water

scruples of Ambergreice with one of musk tye them in a rag with
a bit of lead to make them sink hang it in the midst of the
spirit & set the glass in hott water til the spirit have disolv-
ed the Amber & musk stop the glasse with a cork stoped with
hard wax or dip the cork in hard wax a drop of this will perfume
a quart of any Liquor

mentioned & with grated bread or Bean Flower
boyle them in claret wine to the consistance of a
poltis & lay it on hott to the part & every i2 how-
ers renew it againe tell the Dead flesh seperate
from the quick & then the danger is past

X For a Bearing downe of the Mother M

An skiming dish made of Ashen wood boyle

Lime Water

Take a pound & a halfe of unslackt lime put it to a
Galon of spring water let it stand 8 or i0 howers
& ther will be a thinn cream upon it wch yu must
scim ofe & cast away then take up the cleer
water softly & put into each galon of water
english Liquorish Annis seeds sasafras wood each a
qr of a pound mace a qr of an ounce blew cur-
rance beaten one pound infuse all thes in the
water one night then power ofe the Liquor from
the ingredients Bottle it up for your vse you may
vse only halfe the ingredients to the same quan-
tity of Lime water leting them infuse but one
night longer if it binds adde a sponful or two
of syrope of violets in each draught take it 3
times a day a qr of a pint at a time warmd a
little an hower before breakfast dinner & supper
walk gently after it.
Its excellent against all manner of obstructions
Kings euel & all vlcers inward or outward it
may be used by Injection or wash any vlcer tis
good against any stone m the Bladder or Kidneys.
it clenseth or increaceth bloud drys up super-
fluous humors in the body but not the Bloud
strengtheneth nature purgeth by vrin helpeth
shortnes of breath procureth Appitite killeth
wormes cureth consumptions & Dropsyes is admi-
rable agaimst the cold Palsy if it cause bleeding
feare it not for better will come.

A qr of an ounce of Hira picra put it into a
quart of whitewine let it stand 2 dayes shake-
ing it every day take of this 3 sponfuls fasting
eate not in two howers & repeat the same at 4
a clock when you have taken it all dry some Tares
(such as pigions eate) serce them take a handsom
sponful & after it a qr of a pint of white wine
or halfe a pint if ther stomach will bear it take
it fasting vseing exersise doe  this 9 mornings be
sure the Tares be right

A powder for the Wormes

Worm seed 4 ounces senne an ounce coriander
seed prepared halfe a dram ruberb halfe an ounce
Dryed Rew 2 drams all this made into Powder
give from i0 grains to a Dram in suger or new
wort or any syrope to a child from 6 to 9 years
as much as will lye on a shilling

for those that come in extremity & in Flouds but
not Lyeing Inn weomen

Take red stinging nettles beat them in a morter &
squess out the Iuyce in an Almon presse then set it
on the fire to simmer & scum it well then when it
is cold Bottle it & drink 3 times a day 4 ounces at
a time when they are weary of that doe plantain
the same way & take of that in like manner
they may take more then 4 ounces

Dr Coladon

For Cancers Wounds & the Kings Euel
filthy sores & ulcers & a Fistula +

Take Sanacle Buglos Comphry & Plantaine
each 3 Handfuls Scabious Agrimony Auins
wood Bittony Daysye Rootes & Leaves Bram-
ble Leaves speedwell mugwort pimpernel
Chervil Ribwort Dentdeleon each a hand-

ful wormwood halfe a handful wash a pick
them Clean Shred them & put them into
a Pott with a cover past it up with Rye
past boyle it Leasurely for the space of
4 howers straine it out & when it is cold
put it in a vessel when you vse it
put to a qrt of it a qr of a pint of
white wine as much of the best Hony as
will sweeten it to your tast let it be
ouer the fire a little while when it is
Cold Bottle it & Drink a qr of a pint at
6 in the morning A ii before Dinner
& 4 afternoon & goeing to Bed at wch
time alsoe you may wash the sore wth
it this has cured swellings as big as a Goose Egge.

Cinomon water A Dutch way

Steep Cinomon 48 howers in 2 qrts of Barlye water
a qrt of Hock a qrt of Red Rose water I suppose
a 1/2 pound of cinomon brused suffitient
sometimes cinomon steept in old Hock will stop
the Heart Beating





X The Consumption Syrrup, S

Take two quarts of the first running of Ale wort-and boyl
in it, two ounces of Balsam of Tolu, One Ounce of flower of
Brimstone and one ounce of Elacampane root cut thin, put
them in a masline with a Stone to Sink them, let boyl, three
quarters of an houre very gently, then take it off scum
it and add to it two handfulls of ground Ivy dry'd, and an ounce
of Liquorish scliced, a few Carraway seeds bruis'd and a-
handfull of Raisins. and three ounces of conserve of red
Roses boyl it to a quart then take it off and straine it
and boyl it to syrrup, with white sugar Candy powdered,
let the party take a Spoonfull in the morning before
dinner, at noone and at night;

you may add a little syrrup of Rasps
for the taste sake if you please;

X H  For a Horse sick of the Plague in the
Gutts

two quarts of muscadine a pint & a half of
Angelico water a handful of Rue green & one
of mithradate an ounce of Treacle good store
of Long pepper Liquorish raisins & Ginger
let this be boyld to a qrt give it to a Horse at

up for an hower wrap him warme with clothes
Keep him from the Ayr tel he be well & let him
have warme mashes with mault
